# Reconciled acceptance and original custody follow-ups

## Source and publication boundary

This continuation publishes after the independent documentation commit
`c6ca02296ab46713b3f79dadfb44b5af3577f1c6`, whose only change is
[plan 28](28-exact-head-acceptance-followups-2026-11-15.md). Its executable
preimages are unchanged from merge `61e62d6965cab8d8e6ce6975b34ce80723df6b85`
on Draft PR [#1192](https://github.com/777genius/agent-teams-ai/pull/1192).
Its parents are our acceptance correction
`b3f29d45425dd19cd2dc08c366f27e3d1fbb523d` and the independently prepared
current-frontend integration `b978fce66e54c2fbe3af0cef0d61b79c7f2c5916`.
Preserve both histories and the current frontend snapshot described in
[plan 26](26-current-frontend-reconciliation-2026-11-15.md).

The base remains #1186, `def734338f13bcc3209e78079a0cedbc48f05a5b`.
The checked provider source remains
`421fee918811b8a0b0c73d0ff3e761b9a1c5ecb0`; the pinned archive SHA-256 is
`91350f829b72f83e613505bb444ff0d9ef98fb0835e3e438b918bbea8d930f94`.

The branch is Draft. No main merge, release, default activation or Stage 2
advancement is part of this cut. Real Claude Code and Codex are forbidden,
including version invocations and native binaries pointed at localhost.
Finite protocol peers and the original Host/Main/Electron TEST composition
remain the required substitutes. OpenCode local/native checks retain their
existing allowed scope.

## Reconciliation and residual changes

The merge already contains the original OpenCode prospective destination fix,
Runtime-derived saved-import probes, the shared pinned finite auth-status peer,
the private cold-PATH installer alias and the reserved late native read-failure
observation. These changes were read from the exact merge source and preserved;
the pending parallel proposals were not copied over them.

| Existing owner | Residual correction and retained proof |
| --- | --- |
| Saved-import TEST wrapper | Extract the current wrapper generator so the existing unit exercises the same role checks, exact helper hashes and shared auth dispatch as Desktop. The generated wrapper body is unchanged. |
| Saved-import finite peer | Bind metadata to management and a named saved-model probe to Runtime in both directions. A management-role model probe is refused before protocol input; Runtime auth and metadata remain refused. |
| Import evidence | Check the original `team-probe-` digest namespace, configuration revision, provider and model context. Join every child event to one original birth, require one Runtime input/terminal per probe and preserve the exact session, input, model, effort and terminal checks. The existing auth child has exactly its actual two events and command; no synthetic direct command-ID/PID join is claimed. |
| Import workflow | Permit Runtime effects only for saved import, then run the stronger original import observer. Other management scenarios, Host-parent proof, executable pins, every-child cleanup, empty runtime-session inventory and no extra business captures remain required. |
| Codex installer fixture | Before replacing the generated private sentinel, verify its original digest and canonical parent. After Update, require the exact private alias, symlink target, canonical source A and A digest; retain that proof in the original receipt. No environment override or changed managed-B precedence. |
| Original resolver/path tests | Exercise cold invalid-sentinel refusal, canonical A alias selection and managed B through the existing resolver. A separate existing path-merge test proves the actual private-vendor-before-PATH ordering. Only the native version port is mocked; no vendor binary is run. |
| Main OpenCode regression | Strengthen the existing destination cases with original member identity, saved-model and diagnostic controls, exact returned selections, cold same-ID reconstruction and unchanged view. Existing Claude/Codex controls remain. The Host regression additionally asserts the original product roster revision already captured for the exact prospective destination. No Main/Host authority condition is changed. |
| Original CDP observation | Classify the already-read exact helper basenames `node-exec` and `linux-child-exec` in the closed diagnostic vocabulary. Preserve the strict one-holder oracle and original read/birth guards; classification is not attestation. |
| Original Windows cleanup | Before the existing assertion, expose only the retained slot/source reference and closed preparation/cleanup booleans. Preserve the original root/witness wait, Job cleanup and missing-handle failure. |
| Teams creation E2E | Use explicit finite native metadata peers for Codex and Claude plus a private initial Codex manifest/config directory. This fences both binary resolution and installer status, whose PATH search ignores the native override. Each generated peer accepts only its finite version and refuses business/auth commands. Existing orchestrator fixture, UI assertions, HOME/PATH and staged runtime remain unchanged. |
| Original Windows controller | Save the existing pipeline's numeric exit code immediately and print a fixed provider-labelled diagnostic before the same failure. No C++ actor, deadline or native operation changes. |

The passive emitter/receiver in the merge already reserves the first valid late
read-cut failure. It trades one ordinary row for that reservation and keeps
the original 257-row, 64-KiB and 513-byte limits, including across Host epochs.
The existing regression already covers invalid and duplicate late cuts,
saturation and privacy. No duplicate edit is needed.

## Teams creation workflow environment

The preserved upstream Teams creation workflow runs on Draft PRs. Its original
orchestrator override does not govern native Codex discovery: manual provider
refresh reaches `CodexBinaryResolver`, and installer status independently checks
its managed manifest before searching ambient PATH. Either route could invoke a
runner-installed Codex version command. This is a source-confirmed reachable path;
there is no evidence here that a real vendor binary was installed or executed.

The residual fixture explicitly selects generated finite metadata peers for both
native providers and seeds only its disposable user-data Codex manifest so the
installer status never needs that ambient search. Direct positive/refusal checks
execute only each newly generated finite script through the current Node. The
managed seed is initial TEST state, not an installation result or a Host receipt.
Provider metadata effects have their own fixture ledger; the original orchestrator
lifecycle ledger and all original UI assertions remain intact. No application
HOME/PATH or terminal-runtime bootstrap selection is changed.

A direct workflow cancellation operation was unavailable in the GitHub connector.
The browser fallback also reported no originating connected browser context.
Publication uses the workflow's existing same-PR concurrency cancellation; no
unsupported API mutation or permission workaround is used.

## Original ModelProbe cancellation and admitted HTTP

A separate source review found a concrete cancellation gap in
`OpenCodeV2ModelProbe`. Its original Host request lease starts before native
custody measurement and ends only when D finishes the admitted HTTP operation.
Host close fences admission and aborts the probe, then drains those leases before
closing the D Session. The D Session's transport has its own cancellation signal.
Its resolved driver configuration previously forwarded neither the original probe
signal nor the original close signal to an already-admitted native HTTP request.
A request waiting for headers could therefore retain the lease until the Host's
unchanged five-second drain failed.

The existing `fetchImpl` configuration seam now forwards the original request and
init unchanged, while combining the captured start signal, local close signal
and D's original init signal. D invokes this fetch only after its original native
admission completes. Pending custody still follows its original drain; a late
native callback, failed body/transport release or failed physical stop cannot
be relabelled as confirmed cleanup. No deadline, admission check, native operation,
public API, result/error conversion or cleanup assertion changes.

The new `hostOpenCodeV2ModelProbeLifecycle.test.ts` exercises the real ModelProbe,
both real pinned D clients and global fetch against one finite loopback HTTP
server. Only native process creation/measurement is a controlled TEST
precondition. The first original D log preflight is held before response headers,
before any prompt POST or acknowledged handoff. The four cases require:

- Both original close and parent-signal cancellation close that actual unfinished
  response before waiting for its lease, with no new request/password read.
- A pending original native-custody observation remains UNKNOWN after the
  unchanged five-second drain; a late release cannot repair the cached result.
- Confirmed HTTP cancellation does not repair an unknown original physical stop.

The response-close witness requires an unfinished ServerResponse; ordinary
IncomingMessage completion is not used as an abort witness. Original run/close
settlement, no prompt POST or replay, one original session and one idempotent
physical stop are retained. These are finite integration regressions, not native
process or credential qualification. Source and all four cases received
independent review; no local execution is claimed.

This dataflow correction does not establish the cause of the earlier Linux
native credential failure. Its existing original native receipt and timeout
scenario remain necessary to resolve that separate failure.

The four finite lifecycle cases are appended to the existing retained-Main Vitest
invocation in the process workflow. The same five OS/architecture tuples,
Node pin, single-worker execution, default test budget and original job deadline
remain. No additional workflow, matrix, native opt-in or diagnostic dispatch
is introduced. The complete safe frontend inventory also includes this file.

### Original unrelated sentinel lifetime

The source controller started at 01:45:43.576 UTC and the retained-cleanup
assertion ran at 01:46:48.644. The unchanged ordinary fixture mode sleeps
60 seconds and exits zero. Its expiration is therefore distinct from a scoped
Stop reaching an unrelated child. The assertion follows the completed original
`first.close()`; it cannot make an expired sentinel a valid scope witness.

Only the already-validated `--test-source-provider` branch now selects
`source-sentinel` with its original piped stdin. That exact two-argument actor
requires existing Job membership, emits the same readiness marker and holds
only that original pipe. Any byte is refused; only parent EOF can finish it
normally. The original captured ChildProcess kill and exit wait still own final
cleanup, and the existing 120-second outer kill-on-close Job remains its bound.
Ordinary and vendor actor bodies, all unrelated-alive assertions, 15-second
native waits and source readiness deadlines are unchanged. No PID is reopened
or rediscovered and no new observer process is added.

Controller exit 103 is separate. Its existing order requires original root exit,
fresh zero Job membership and successful Job-handle closure before filesystem
removal. The removal error still fails with 103. Only that retained
`std::error_code` numeric value and a closed system/generic/other category are
now printed, together with the already-retained root exit-code variable and
emergency flag. Under emergency=true, that variable may retain the original
initial value 99; it is not a fresh post-kill exit observation. No filesystem
retry, permission change, path/message disclosure or additional probe is added. The actual deletion cause remains unproven until
that original failure yields its error code.

### One owner for nested Settings evidence

The actual producer assigns Settings evidence to
`mixedCreateLaunchProof.providerSettings`. The canonical failure projection
already reads that owner, but the final receipt summary had a 126-line duplicate
that instead read a root-level Settings record. The duplicate is removed and
the existing canonical function is imported and re-exported without changing
its output fields or 4096-byte bound.

One regression in the existing admitted Settings test file calls the actual
final summary. A conflicting root-level PASS record cannot replace the nested
INCOMPLETE proof, and an absent nested owner remains absent. The test also
checks getter refusal, redaction, unchanged original evidence, fresh projected
arrays/rows, and omission for successful or non-mixed summaries. This corrects
lost diagnostic evidence; it does not change UI acceptance or cleanup outcomes.

These residual source/test changes received independent review. Their existing
Windows builds/source-provider scenarios and Desktop prerequisite test remain
the validation gates; no local execution is claimed while the executor is
offline. No workflow, matrix, timeout or native opt-in is added by this cut.

## Preserve the independently executed finite-probe correction

Before this residual publication, the branch advanced to merge
`bc4ae63b7ad712c851fe2da23511e94b026347e6`, with parents `01349f7e` and
`862a5defdf0582cdc01994168611d4f328cc47b0`. Its exact five-path delta was
read and independently reviewed. It already narrows the TEST peer's ephemeral
assertion to its explicit standalone-MCP branch and adds actual Host/D probes
for Default and Low. Both earlier standalone refusal cases and all other
original finite tests remain. The separate owner's executed before/after
results, scoped compiler/lint checks and incomplete full compiler exit 137 are
recorded with their source and limits in [plan 28](28-exact-head-acceptance-followups-2026-11-15.md);
they are not local execution by this continuation.

The unused parallel candidate was discarded. Preserve the external actual
Host tests, its bounded first ambiguous Windows entry-match projection and
the Teams cleanup loop's equivalent `for (;;)` lint correction. No duplicate
probe test or alternative matchset filter is introduced.

One residual observation correction checks the existing monotonic five-second
deadline after the final original probe read. Otherwise a completion arriving
after the while-loop budget could be accepted by that last read. It adds no
read, request, retry, owner or timeout; late completion now fails the same
original observation bound.

### Same original sandbox, extended Windows path spelling

The source chain is concrete: prepareEmulatedSourceProfile passes its original
storageRoot through TeamHost/RuntimeTeamCoordinator into FileRuntimeStore.
The original runtime receipt is below storage/runtime-sessions, then a
64-character member digest and a 64-character binding digest plus .json.
Constructing that path from the observed 48-character short sandbox spelling
already yields 267 characters. The source profile also calls realpathSync.native,
which can expand the short user-directory alias; 267 describes that constructed spelling,
not inspection of the actual surviving file.

The controller is built without a longPathAware manifest. Its original
remove_all received the plain sandbox path. Microsoft's
[long-path documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/fileio/maximum-file-path-limitation)
requires extended drive/UNC spelling or the corresponding per-process opt-in.
[GetTempPathW](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-gettemppathw)
supplies the properly formatted fully qualified original temp path and retains
its symlinks. Current Microsoft/STL filesystem source corroborates that raw
paths reach Win32 and a child not-found result can be followed by a surviving
directory-not-empty error; that source is not an identification of the exact
runner STL binary.

Only the original final cleanup argument changes. Already explicit \\?\ and
\\.\ namespaces remain byte-for-byte unchanged; an ordinary UNC or drive
spelling of the same selected sandbox gains its extended namespace. The
original selected runtime path/argv, source hashes, root handle, fresh Job
accounting, successful CloseHandle, single remove_all and all error/103/root-code
decisions remain. There is no extra lookup, canonicalization, retry, chmod,
timeout or permission repair. The existing original source gate's long
persistent receipts provide the native regression. Another removal failure
still fails; the new spelling alone is not a demonstrated fix for every error 145.

allocation. These observations cannot qualify an unprepared slot or choose
a speculative performance correction.

## 2026-11-15 captured V1 OAuth presentation and original-gate corrections

This reviewed continuation builds on exact source95da89d786546bf1bb2afe4c047afe3e4dba78ad.

introduced.

### Observed frontend gap and implementation

The original provider-settings renderer already renders and copies a device code
from native instructions, applies its existing copyable authorization-link policy
and offers Cancel while waiting for the browser. CapturedProviderOAuth previously
discarded the native instructions and mapped a still-pending automatic native
callback to the public completing phase. That hid the device code and disabled
the existing cancellation controls.

The three existing presentation functions are moved byte-for-byte, apart from
their export keywords, from AgentTeamsRuntimeProviderManagementCliClient into
runtimeProviderOAuthPresentation. The legacy CLI client and captured OpenCode V1
path now share the original text sanitizer,1000-character instruction projection,
8192-character public URL check and provider/path-specific copyable-link policy.
This is not a second URL policy. The private16384-character authorization guard
and original external opener remain unchanged.

Main keeps only the sanitized presentation in the original running operation.
After the once-only opener, a fresh oauth.read must still match the original
operation/profile/configuration/method, monotonic sequence and fixed expiry.
Currentness and that same expiry are checked before and after the await.
Cancellation, completion or a context switch that wins while the opener waits
prevents late presentation. The existing automatic callback is submitted once;
while it remains pending, public progress stays waiting-for-browser with the
sanitized instructions and existing Cancel control. Manual/terminal progress
clears presentation. Durable Main intents and Host command records receive no
native instruction, device code or authorization URL.

The presentation and automatic-phase mapping apply to captured OpenCode V1.
Captured Claude behavior is preserved. OpenCode V2 keeps its original API-key-only
provider-management boundary; this change adds no V2 OAuth protocol.

## Preserve original native provider deadlines and observe the captured HANDLE

A read-only audit of the existing PR1192 review threads found two still-current acceptance issues in `tools/owned-process-broker/nativeEmulatedProviderGate.ts`, exact preimage `febe19c803db4391dfebb6716855b4ed855d115e` (225 lines). This is the default finite-provider scenario, separate from nativeEmulatedSourceScenario's A/B branch. Thread4568304969 correctly identifies input/Stop loops that accept terminal/completed before their deadline check. The private rpc currently gives every read its fresh profile15-second allowance, although the original Stop phase has10 seconds. A late successful reply can therefore pass an expired phase. This is a source-confirmed oracle gap, not an observed95 execution failure.

Thread4567946504 concerns the same scenario's three retained witnesses. Default capturedWitness starts its native15-second wait as soon as capture is acknowledged; both sequential Stops each already permit10 seconds. Thus individually valid phases can outlast that observer wait, and a witness process's exitCode is not a fresh observation of the target HANDLE. Retain the same original native target identities, captures and custody rather than increasing a timer or choosing an unbounded vendor actor.

SOURCE PLAN is limited to that one existing TEST file. Its local rpc may accept an optional absolute phase cutoff, check it before dispatch and after the original await before accepting any success, and pass its decreasing remainder through the existing CapturedHostClient.request remainingMs option. That existing client floors and validates the remainder and caps its original profile timeout. Apply it only to the input runtime.read loop and each Stop's one original terminate plus subsequent same-command lookups. The input15-second cutoff still begins after the original team.execute; each Stop10-second cutoff still begins before its original terminate. Preserve every command id, payload, original nonterminal check,25ms delay, initial open/ready/list and subsequent evidence phase. No deadline starts over, no success after the cutoff is accepted, and no business action or retry is added.

Reuse existing capturedQueryableWitness at exactly the same three capture points, explicitly selecting the already available non-vendor query-wait-gated mode. It checks original Job containment and captures the same PID/birth HANDLE once. Do not alter nativeGateContext, the C++ actor, selected profile, child count, helper defaults, workflow or controller. Replace only the three old survivor exitCode reads with fresh Q observations on the existing HANDLE: neighbor once after Stop1, provider root after each Stop. Add no initial query. Replace the three original absence waits with the helper's memoized W/wait at their original locations: each service after its own Stop, and provider root after shutdown. Require alive for the same survivor predicates and exit0 for the same absence predicates. Maximum actual queries are two for root, one for neighbor and zero for primary; the existing per-HANDLE cap4 remains. No PID reopen, signal or new target/observer child is introduced.

Retain the existing external10-second bound for each observation/wait and the single10-second final aggregate. Use async per-witness callbacks in that aggregate so a synchronous wait refusal does not prevent attempting the other already-captured witnesses; require each returned exit code0. The helper memoizes W, so completed waits are not replayed. Original Host shutdown/native physical close still precede observer settlement, and the existing fallback kills only the already tracked observer child objects. Missing capture, failed query/wait, active or unresolved original custody remains failure/unknown, never synthetic cleanup.

The protocol change intentionally moves the ordinary native15-second wait's start from initial capture ACK to explicit W at the original absence check. The observer can therefore retain its HANDLE longer from initial capture. It remains within the unchanged original120-second controller Job; the native wait duration, external observation bounds, leaf/sentinel lifetimes and child count do not increase. No vendor opt-in or INFINITE wait is selected. This states the actual observation-lifetime change rather than claiming unchanged capture-to-exit timing.

### First original launch refusal

In HostWindowsLaunch, observe only the existing RemotePending captureSpec/stage/prepare/resume/assertInput/failed boundaries and the original requestWhenReady result in HostWindowsLaunch.request. One first-failure latch belongs to each existing pending allocation and has no business meaning. A private diagnostics_channel publishes only when subscribed; there are no success events.

The native record has a fixed schema and includes only: kind, original capability UUID, closed phase, closed reason, and the already-held booleans dispatched/staged/rootCaptured. Phase is one of capture, staging, allocate, launch-chunk, launch-commit, post-commit, prepare, post-prepare or resume. Reason is one of sealed, current-false, current-threw, launch-deadline, refused, unknown, result-mismatch or preparation-failed. A deadline-expired fact is true only when the original deadline predicate itself rejected; otherwise it is null. Do not emit exceptions, messages, stack traces, executable/configuration/environment/argv data, paths, credentials or raw process IO.

Preserve the exact short-circuit order of assertInput: sealed first; current() exactly once only if unsealed; the existing performance.now() comparison only if current passed and preparing is true. A thrown current() error remains the same thrown object. The existing request builder is still passed/invoked once under the same admission and deadline. An optional passive refusal observer may describe its original refused/unknown/mismatched reply before the unchanged unavailable throw; other callers retain the original signature behavior. Mark phases without repeating any operation or assertion. failed() retains its original Preparation shape and reason and only supplies a closed fallback diagnostic when no earlier fact exists. Subscriber outcomes cannot select a retry, change the reply or satisfy preparation.

### First original consumer and retained-reference refusal

In prepareTeamModelReadiness.inspectMemberModelReadiness, observe the existing current() checks in order: initial authority, original consumer read, consumer/configuration identity, continuation, required management/reference/cutoff, consumer cutoff, retained reference, reference equality, final authority. Keep a first-refusal latch in the existing captured consumer closure. The consumer record includes kind, digests of original action/group/binding identifiers, original consumerId, the closed phase/reason, and only the cutoff/clock values actually evaluated by the original predicate. Missing observations are null. It neither rereads a store nor calls another guard to explain failure.

In ModelProbeOwner.captureReadinessReference, distinguish the actual original admission/target rejection, missing scope, retired reference, not-completed/not-ok/resource-held, probe expiry, scope cutoff expiry, preparation expiry, origin-not-current, purpose/readiness/account mismatch, and final projection rejection. The reference record includes kind, original command/context digests, the closed phase/reason and original evaluated expiry/clock facts. It links to the consumer's original group command; it is not a replacement reference.

Pure compound predicates may be split only to name the predicate that actually rejected. Preserve their left-to-right short-circuit behavior and exact assertAdmission/assertManagementTarget/inspect/current/Date.now call counts and ordering. Capture the return of an existing clock call instead of adding another; expiry checks that were skipped remain skipped. No extra Date.now/current()/filesystem/network read occurs on success for diagnosis. An observed original exception is rethrown unchanged. Original generic public errors, durable records, ownership checks and cutoff values remain unchanged. A later expired persisted record is not proof that expiry caused the earlier refusal; do not infer that cause from post-failure state.

### Bounded assessment of a pre-exec retained task-port proposal

A further read-only assessment considered whether the original child could transfer a task-read/self port before its original exec, allowing the parent to retain VM-read access to the selected image afterward. The current captured-child/direct posix_spawn topology contains no such handoff, and its existing task-name/audit-token observation does not supply VM-read authority. Even granting a hypothetical pre-exec handoff, the already-pinned [XNU kern_exec.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/bsd/kern/kern_exec.c), blob 47420f9f8f620233f112e87a51ed8f576caa3546, creates a replacement proc/task/thread for ordinary exec and terminates the old task before releasing the new exec thread. The corresponding [task.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/osfmk/kern/task.c), blob 205ac1358dc60a8e98f13de1219c41b8565a1cc9, calls ipc_task_disable during termination; [ipc_tt.c](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/osfmk/kern/ipc_tt.c), blob c510efeeea41687d19bd18c9e226194bc854c4ff, disables the old control/read kobjects. An externally retained old send right does not become the replacement task's read capability. This refutes this candidate's persistence premise under the pinned source; it is not a new native observation, a runtime-kernel measurement, or a universal impossibility claim.

Obtaining a different post-exec capability would be another proposal and would still require complete consumption/currentness proof. Reading specified virtual ranges is an observation; it does not by itself establish complete selected-image equality, stability of later page consumption, or binding of the original selected script's later reads. The pinned native mappings and delayed/private COW semantics also do not justify claiming that every later fault must consume changed bytes. The original script behavior remains decisive for compatibility: the existing test changes its own source through $0, then requires the original source-changed publication refusal after execution. A lifetime write freeze, changed path/$0 behavior, excluded writer or alternate source would change that contract. Do not advance the rejected retained-port candidate to code or infer user permission to alter these semantics. P09 remains open under its original obligations; no source, experiment, native observation, writer authority, supported source class, oracle or qualification changes through this record.

### Proposed R1 — Deliver current Host roster readiness with the existing progress observation

Status: exact review proposal only; no tracked source/test edit, candidate execution, provider/native run or CI rerun is authorized by this draft. This standalone appendix supersedes the unpublished seven-path draft and its one-off Add status read. Append after the current verified Plan29 prefix at `c7ab44bf842dd12d0dadfd6ac2f7fc547e93c516`, document blob `c33289984a64ccdf3c0c9eb060374cdb8ef7ef9d`; preserve any subsequently published evidence prefix too. All ten preimages below are unchanged from that clean source. Do not replace the full Plan29 with an older draft.

**Original evidence and its limits.** Collaboration job `132812543188`, run `43714348666`, clean source `665599512298f6de64c73ebbfe325166647d13ff`, official artifact `14163729668` (1,444,039 bytes; SHA256 `5b8d2efc82dde09fc2b7d34c81f880e6a16bf4926d35a3707685b38a2946e5bb`) completed the original finite-peer actions and physical cleanup. Receipt SHA256 is `899ba1381ecf33eb3b61c6de32795b05afccdf9c8af286093fa1ee0e87b790d9`. The launch PNG shows all one initial teammate joined. The group PNG SHA256 `82db240ce29657e311e37560f1502b0722552aa154524d1d9e0c79291a2db7cd` and restored PNG SHA256 `a0445619c67c9796903bc33bc75774beadc3db4a871a6ad1126e600dab375b7c` instead show “Finishing launch / 1 teammate still joining”, with the three members and their original group/restored replies visible. Add and Restore retain the original launch ID and select distinct Carol generations; the captured sessions report nativeReady and the original subsequent replies complete. The receipt does not retain the exact aggregate launch configuration, renderer progress/spawn transition or response order at those frames. These images establish the visible discrepancy; they do not prove that one particular store interleaving occurred or that every aggregate bootstrap predicate was ready at capture. The source counterexample below is the repair basis. Historical PASS/failure classifications and native-provider qualification remain unchanged.

**Reproducible source counterexample.** `liveRosterMembership.publish` selects the live member and recomputes `definition.rosterRevision` without changing `launchId`; `TeamLaunchOwner.summary` exposes the current revision and attempts. Both existing Host projections preserve the full canonical `[handleId, launchId, rosterRevision]` run ID. Start with a valid ready Alice/Bob R0 and Bob online/confirmed, then observe a valid current ready Alice/Bob/Carol R1 for the same Host and launch. The original runtime resolver refuses the changed run ID because Bob is still online. The progress successor is also refused because runtime remains R0 and the members are not fully stopped. The separately refreshed Team roster contains Carol, so the shared join calculation sees two expected teammates but only Bob confirmed and renders exactly “Finishing launch / 1 teammate still joining”. Repeating a current R1 read does not overcome this guard.

This transition is also allowed while the predecessor is assembling/partial: existing live-member admission requires the captured attached/allocated/owned lead and retained source, not aggregate terminal readiness. A snapshot-only fix followed by the old terminal-only successor rule therefore leaves a second lock. A same-handle/launch exception without provenance is unsafe: the roster digest is not a chronological sequence, and an unpinned delayed R1 may arrive after R2 selects a restored generation. No timestamp or digest ordering heuristic is proposed. The full tuple stays intact because captured Cancel/Stop validates its exact revision and member generations. The audited compensation/remove paths retain the selected launch definition/revision; the validation predecessor is a clone. Add/Restore use fresh generation/binding inputs and Restart/Resume advance generation; no legal earlier launch digest return was found in those paths. Retirement applies only to an actually displaced exact tuple.

**Existing liveness channel.** A settled Bob-only spawn snapshot can stop `TeamSpawnStatusWatcher`; its dependencies do not include the newly refreshed roster. A one-off post-Add member read can also be superseded by the existing progress read, leaving that settled snapshot unchanged. This proposal adds no Add read and does not modify Add, Restore or the watcher. The existing global provisioning subscription remains registered after ready, failed and Stop. While it is registered, `createHostTeamChanges` continues its original nonoverlapping two-second poll and reads complete launch configuration; the existing progress signature includes the full run ID, so a revision change is already a change even when aggregate progress remains ready. The already-read configuration is sufficient to project both current progress and member readiness without another RPC.

**Exact-object provenance and delivery.** In the existing Host member-spawn adapter module, private WeakMaps retain the actual projected snapshot object with its original canonical Team key, full run ID and existing scope/read-fence check, and associate an actual progress object with that companion snapshot and captured Team/run tuple. They add no wire/DTO fields, control capability, independent ordering counter, timer or RPC. Registration is internal to the feature; only read-only inspection functions are exported through its existing renderer facade. The maps retain references to these delivered DTOs, not a second authoritative Team store. An absent configuration keeps the original unregistered `{ statuses: {}, runId: null }` result. Purely projected/plain DTOs and later clones receive no registration.

The direct spawn reader preserves its original request and shared launch-read checks before return. Its post-return check reuses the captured scope predicate plus the surviving existing `launchReadFence`; it does not reuse the per-adapter `current()` entry deleted by the original finally. Direct `getProvisioningStatus` projects the companion from its already-validated configuration and retains `selected.assertCurrent`, whose scope/request/read-order checks remain valid after return. Its exact launch/revision validation and the entire Cancel/Stop path remain unchanged.

The shared observer projects the same companion from the same configuration already read for progress. Each listener still receives its own structured clone of progress and now its own companion clone; registration is on that exact delivered progress object. A listener's mutation of its clone cannot alter another listener's projection. The companion lookup distinguishes unregistered progress (`undefined`) from a known stale or tuple-changed Host object (`null`); a known stale object is refused as a whole. It verifies both `progress.teamName` and `progress.runId` against the retained tuple and rechecks the companion's currentness. Cloning a DTO or changing its owner/run ID cannot acquire this handoff authority.

**Signature commit and convergence.** The existing signature expression, including the omission of observation time and diagnostic observedAt, remains unchanged. Its commit moves from before the synchronous listener loop to after that loop and a recheck of the same active scope/subscription epoch and shared launch-read fence. If a listener causes a mutation/read/scope invalidation during delivery, the changed signature is not committed; the next original two-second iteration can deliver the same current projection again. There is no new retry, timer, acknowledgement DTO, source read or native effect. The original callback exception isolation remains. This honestly changes when a signature is considered delivered: a listener that applied before another synchronous invalidation may receive a duplicate on the next original cycle; the existing store equality rules suppress equivalent state. No claim is made that arbitrary throwing consumers must be retried indefinitely. Configuration and ordinary feed read counts/cadence are unchanged; progress-only tests assert one configuration RPC per existing iteration.

**Store transition and refusals.** The original progress handler first retains both ignored-run checks and the startedAt floor. It then obtains the exact companion, refuses known-stale progress, applies the existing shared member-spawn projection, and rechecks that same companion after synchronous store notifications before continuing the original progress handling. Thus a store subscriber that invalidates the observation between snapshot application and progress application cannot commit that stale progress. No new explicit launch/Stop authority or generation selection is introduced.

`projectIncomingMemberSpawnSnapshot` rechecks registered currentness before generic stopped fallback and before semantic-equality suppression. Only a current original snapshot can move a canonical same-Host/same-launch pin to a different roster revision; the current and incoming exact tuples must be absent from both existing ignore maps. Unregistered same-launch revisions are refused even with offline current members. The new state retains only the already-projected statuses/full next run ID and retires the exact previous tuple in the existing `ignoredRuntimeRunIds`, blocking late previous progress from repinning it. Registered skipped R1 observations are refused after a newer R2 read even when R1 was never pinned or tombstoned. Current Restore generations may be pending; no status is promoted to ready.

The generic `resolveIncomingRuntimeRunAdoption` function remains byte-identical. For a different revision of the same canonical Host launch, `shouldAdoptSuccessorProvisioningRun` exclusively requires `currentRuntimeRunId === nextRunId` before the old generic terminal/stopped fallback. This accepts nonterminal-predecessor progress only after the actual snapshot has pinned that exact next tuple, and blocks plain/late same-launch progress from using the all-offline fallback. Other legacy/new-launch behavior, full ID encoding/parsing, shared `teamProvisioningStateRules`, selection/context/mutation guards and lifecycle controls remain unchanged.

**Original Collaboration UI oracle.** Extend only the existing private `visible(text)` helper with an opt-in joined condition, enabled at the two original waits for `TEST collaboration Carol reply` after Add and `TEST collaboration Carol restored` after Restore. The original body-text predicate remains required in the same `waitRead` expression and the same 60-second budget. The additional DOM condition requires exactly one visible content Team for the canonical `hostTeamSelectionKey(fixture.handleId)`, exactly one of its direct progress wrappers containing the original Copy diagnostics/Diagnostics copied control, a visible paragraph equal to `Team launched - all 2 teammates joined`, and no `Finishing launch` or `still joining` text in that wrapper. An absent/hidden progress block is not success. The original finite fixture has the same Bob/Carol active roster at both points; its source/member/generation/reply assertions remain intact. This UI condition proves the rendered readiness presentation, not a generation ID embedded in the DOM. Existing source/receipt assertions continue to qualify the generations.

No new CDP observation call, native input, action replay, polling primitive or deadline is added. The same wait may take more of its original iterations while presentation converges. All other visible waits, including offline/history reload, retain the original expression, and the existing direct/restored screenshots remain at their original positions. Group/restored PNGs must still be independently inspected on the eventual published source. The current 02fd final stopped poll, its physical-absence predicate, owner-qualified offline/Launch/no-progress requirement, 75-second bound and all cleanup assertions stay byte-identical.

**Exact ten-path allowlist and physical bounds.** These are exact review candidates and maximum approved deltas if published unchanged; a mismatch requires reconciliation before editing. Every original test line is retained byte-identically in order. Only the listed imports, finite cases and production seams are added or changed. The existing teamSlice ceiling remains 5,020 physical lines; the candidate has 5,012. No size baseline is changed.

**Regression slots and required RED/GREEN sequence.** The existing `hostMemberSpawnStatuses.test.ts` receives 12 finite cases using the actual adapter, shared store projection and presentation: current Add handoff without stopping Bob; R0→R2 with a pending restored generation and delayed unseen R1 refused even offline; post-return context/mutation/new-launch-read invalidation; clone/plain/foreign-launch/foreign-owner/tampered-ID refusal; and both existing ignore maps for predecessor and successor. Root/transport observers and stream forwarding were installed before entering resume. The finite source assessment established no removable computation or await that preserves every original freshness observation, lease/TTL, action/witness count and interleaving contract. ### Runtime activity facade: align three original stopped-run expectations with the published `02fd` contract (R1)

**Observed CI evidence.** Original Host Emulated Acceptance run `43725305996`, safe frontend shard 2 job `132845835405`, checked out and verified `6626e7ac5d516795a5d2c7671c631c6f9c970df5`. Its complete Vitest summary is **541 files: 538 PASS, 1 FAIL, 2 SKIP; 6,740 tests: 6,721 PASS, 1 FAIL, 18 SKIP; 388.03 s**. The only failed assertion is `test/features/agent-backend/runtimeActivityFacade.test.ts:420:64`, in the original case `projects actual NDJSON Runtime activity through the production facade after a canonical Team input`: expected `cancelled`, received `disconnected`. The file reports 7 PASS/1 FAIL; the failed case ran 469.002289 ms. There is no reported timeout failure. Later assertions at lines 654 and 673 were not reached by this failed case; they are not additional observed RED results. This is original CI evidence, not a local RED run on the later combined source.

The original full job log is 200,930 bytes, SHA256 `ff80b09cf7f0c5bb36c5b2c609734ba4e3688508b737a05b9e7aea7d24c7c7ff`. Matching official artifact `14167079709` is 312,946 bytes, SHA256 `3a076edff23f879ce9b7c10e2549013da2193e2e3898286bfdad4b42dc8a6431`, verified against API/upload metadata with both ZIP entries safely extracted and CRC checked. Its original JSON report is 2,382,036 bytes, SHA256 `d4bd01cdcad64befe7e29db249948517e0a970af992c3c62f099dd1ba3aedad4`; the JSON assertion records confirm one failed test and 18 skips. The job first captures Node 24.21.0 as `AGENT_BACKEND_TEST_NODE`, then uses `.node-version` Node **26.12.1** for the frontend Vitest process. These are separate toolchain roles; this is not a claim that the whole shard ran under Node 24.

**Why the expected state changes.** Published commit `02fd2c09d2b549a29910049206884375099723e9` deliberately maps a Host launch whose participating members are `failed` with reason `stopped` to `disconnected`. `projectHostProvisioningProgress.ts` remains blob `24e86d8c5c210bfd3eadbcadbbebcbd71ef4c88d` at both the failed source and the currently inspected source. The unchanged shared `teamProvisioningStateRules.ts` (blob `088f69076851992ecf9030454f666c1a32e2e6c6`) accepts same-run `ready -> disconnected`, rejects `ready -> cancelled`, and treats both as terminal. The existing Host progress regression already checks that actual projector output is accepted by that shared guard. Reverting production to `cancelled` would restore the previously established stale-progress defect.

The first and final current-run assertions follow the actual adapter's `cancelProvisioning`, which delegates to `createHostTeamLifecycle.stopCaptured`. That function retains each original member's `expectedGeneration`, sends `runtime.stop`, and requires a matching result with `attached:false` and `custody:'released'`; refusal or uncertain cleanup throws instead of supplying a success state. The finite session fixture's original `close` resolves its observation and reports confirmed cleanup. In `TeamLaunchOwner.project`, an original Runtime record with `phase:'closed'` and `custody:'released'` becomes `failed`/`stopped`. This bounded R1 follows the incoming Main stream-preservation change without changing its production verifier. The executable baseline is PR head `d382c393898d35c9340cd903d1c02fb868c18a8e`, tree `5daa592058d9cf9016d56c78e119d5b18e8248a8`. All four new streaming cases fail; these are not infrastructure failures and must not be relabelled or rerun to obtain a different classification.

The intact case rejects at `CapturedWindowsCustody.ts:102`, the original canonical-cwd/directory guard, before broker/provider streaming. The changed-tail and replacement cases reach the generic expected refusal but then correctly fail their unchanged `observedRead === true` assertions. The EIO case correctly rejects the different early error instead of accepting it as the injected original EIO. The existing separate three-file protocol suite has53PASS in each log; that result does not qualify the failed Main tests. Original x64 artifact14170801111 is11106bytes/SHA25611fb815130d43ecccd7d9f104f9a01bb6a45ffdaa2fc28323bbcf5901bf69c4a; ARM64 artifact14171316320 is11140bytes/SHA256eddc3a80ed28b1282853cbfe3bade4533390faaf03fde1c77cb92e270ded8499. Original upload lines, direct API metadata, run and head agree. This review retained raw logs and artifact metadata; it does not claim archive extraction.

The fixture obtains its private temporary cwd through ordinary `realpathSync`; Main checks that exact cwd through `fs.promises.realpath`. The Node24.21.0 primary sources distinguish the algorithms: [ordinary/native sync implementations](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v24.21.0/lib/fs.js), [promise implementation](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v24.21.0/lib/internal/fs/promises.js), and [original Windows libuv implementation](https://github.com/nodejs/node/blob/v24.21.0/deps/uv/src/win/fs.c). Ordinary sync walks links in JavaScript; native sync and the promise API use the same binding.realpath implementation, whose Windows path comes from GetFinalPathNameByHandleW. The failed log does not retain the two actual path spellings; no particular short-name, casing or external directory mutation is asserted.
